[CHAT #2] SENDER: assistant | TYPE: text | TIME: 2026-08-29T04:03:17.262Z
I stopped before clicking **Mount bucket** because Hugging Face shows a security warning: attaching this private bucket to the currently **public** Space may make the bucket’s contents publicly accessible. I will not accept that risk for user state, credits, or JSON data.

We need choose a safer architecture:

1. **Do not mount the private bucket to the public Space.** Keep the bucket separate and use Supabase as the durable source after the controlled cutover; this is the safest option.
2. Temporarily make the Space private, attach the bucket, migrate and validate the state, then reassess whether the Space can safely be public. This may make the live app inaccessible to public users during the setup.
3. Use a separate private backend/runtime for bucket access and keep the public Space/frontend isolated. This is more secure but requires additional deployment work.

I recommend **option 1 or 3**. The bucket has been created privately, but it is not mounted, so no data is exposed and the Space remains unchanged. Which option should I implement?

    "contentPreview": "# Hugging Face Spaces persistence findings — 2026-08-28\n\nSource: https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/en/spaces-storage\nTitle: Disk usage on Spaces · Hugging Face\n\nOfficial documentation states that the default disk attached to every Space is ephemeral and its contents are lost when the Space restarts or is stopped. For data that must persist longer than the Space lifecycle, Hugging Face recommends attaching one or more Storage Buckets as volumes.\n\nThe documentation states that attached Storage Buckets are mounted into the Space container at a user-specified path and are available as local files at runtime. Buckets may be mounted read-write by default or read-only. They can be attached from the Space settings UI or through the Hugging Face Hub Python API.\n\nApplication to NepalAI Studio: the current JSON files, generated outputs, and other mutable file-backed state cannot be treated as durable on the default Space disk. A Storage Bucket mounted at a stable path, such as `/data`, would preserve the JSON source across restarts, but media binaries and database metadata should still have separate lifecycle and backup policies. Supabase remains the relational metadata target; the bucket would be a transitional persistent source for legacy JSON until shadow parity and production cutover are complete.\n\n\nSource: https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/en/storage-buckets\nTitle: Storage Buckets · Hugging Face\n\nOfficial documentation describes Storage Buckets as non-versioned, mutable S3-like object storage powered by Xet. They are intended for working storage such as logs, checkpoints, intermediate artifacts, and large mutable file collections. Buckets are available to users and organizations, can be public or private, and can be accessed through the Hub UI, CLI, Python API, S3-compatible tooling, or mounted into a Space.\n\nApplication to NepalAI Studio: a private bucket is a suitable transitional location for the legacy JSON source directory and generated mutable artifacts when the app still requires filesystem semantics. Because buckets are mutable and non-versioned, the app must add its own atomic-write, backup, checksum, and recovery controls. The bucket should not be treated as a substitute for Supabase relational durability or payment/credit transaction integrity.\n\n\nSource: https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/en/storage-buckets-access\nTitle: Access Patterns · Hugging Face\n\nOfficial documentation lists volume mounts in Jobs and Spaces as a managed way to mount buckets read-write, with no extra setup after the bucket is attached. It also lists CLI sync for batch transfers and backups, and S3-compatible access for existing S3 tooling. Bucket data can be mounted at a chosen container path, such as `/data`.\n\nApplication to NepalAI Studio: the cleanest transitional design is a private Storage Bucket attached read-write at `/data`, with the legacy JSON stores moved under `/data/nepalai-state/`. [CHAT #3] SENDER: user | TYPE: text | TIME: 2026-08-31T03:56:59.917Z
what extra things we can implement to make it very premium

--------------------------------------------------
[CHAT #4] SENDER: assistant | TYPE: text | TIME: 2026-08-31T03:57:32.136Z
## Extra features that would make NepalAI Studio truly premium

The biggest upgrade is not simply adding more AI providers. Premium platforms win through a **clear creative workflow, professional output quality, speed, reliability, and confidence**. NepalAI Studio should feel like a guided production suite rather than a collection of separate tools.

### 1. Create a unified “Campaign Studio”

Let a user choose a goal—advertisement, YouTube video, short reel, product promotion, tourism guide, educational explainer, festival greeting, news-style information, or social announcement—and enter one idea. HamroAI should convert it into a complete production plan:

| Generated item | Example output |
|---|---|
| Campaign brief | Audience, objective, tone, CTA |
| Script | Nepali, English, Hindi, or mixed-language script |
| Scene plan | Scene duration, visual prompt, narration, caption |
| Asset plan | Images, logo, product photos, icons, music, overlays |
| Voice plan | Voice, speed, emotion, pronunciation notes |
| Distribution versions | YouTube landscape, Shorts/Reels/TikTok vertical, square social post |

This would make the product much easier for non-technical Nepalese businesses, schools, creators, NGOs, and local government teams.

### 2. Build a professional timeline editor

The Video Studio should have one dominant preview and a timeline directly below it. Add scene cards, drag-and-drop ordering, snap-to-grid alignment, trim handles, duration fields, zoom controls, playhead scrubbing, undo/redo, keyboard shortcuts, and a visible autosave/recovery indicator.

Essential track types should include:

| Track | Features |
|---|---|
| Video/scene | Image, video, transition, crop, pan, zoom, speed |
| Voice | Narration clips, waveform, trim, volume, fade, pronunciation marker |
| Music | Copyright status, ducking under voice, fade in/out, trim |
| Text | Captions, headlines, lower thirds, ticker, CTA, phone number |
| Brand | Logo, watermark, colors, fonts, safe-area positioning |
| Effects | Motion, transition, filters, overlays, stickers, background removal |

Add timeline presets for **YouTube 16:9, Shorts/Reels/TikTok 9:16, square 1:1, and presentation 4:3**.

### 3. Add Nepal-first brand kits

Create reusable brand kits for businesses and organizations:

- Logo upload and automatic transparent-background preparation.
- Nepali and Devanagari-safe font selection.
- Brand colors and gradients.
- Default intro/outro scenes.
- Business name, phone number, address, website, QR code, and social handles.
- Reusable watermark and lower-third templates.
- Festival and local-event templates for Dashain, Tihar, Teej, Holi, New Year, Buddha Jayanti, tourism season, and local municipal campaigns.

A user should be able to create a professional video repeatedly without rebuilding branding every time.

### 4. Make Voice Desk studio-quality

Voice Desk should include a guided narration workflow instead of only provider selection. Add:

| Capability | Premium behavior |
|---|---|
| Pronunciation dictionary | User defines how names, places, and Nepali words are pronounced |
| Voice audition | Generate a short preview before consuming full credits |
| Emotion presets | News, documentary, warm, energetic, formal, devotional, promotional |
| Sentence timing | Show approximate duration before generation |
| Pause controls | Visual pause markers and custom break lengths |
| Loudness normalization | Consistent output level across scenes |
| Music ducking | Automatically lower music during narration |
| Language lock | Prevent mixed-language drift unless explicitly requested |
| Version history | Compare script and voice versions |
| Audio repair | Silence trimming, fade, noise reduction, clipping warning |

The interface should show an estimated duration, estimated credits, provider, and expected completion time before generation.

### 5. Add high-quality visual direction tools

Image creation should accept both text and reference images. Premium controls should include:

- Reference image upload.
- Image-to-image strength.
- Character and product consistency across scenes.
- Fixed aspect-ratio presets.
- Camera angle, lens, lighting, time of day, and composition controls.
- Negative prompts and safety filters.
- Seed and variation controls where supported.
- “Generate 4 variations” with side-by-side comparison.
- Upscale, face/detail repair, background removal, and transparent PNG export.
- A visual continuity panel showing the selected style across a campaign.

For story and advertisement creation, generate a **shot list** rather than unrelated images. Each scene should retain style, characters, wardrobe, location, and lighting notes.

### 6. Add a reusable asset library

The Asset Library should become the central source for every project. Include:

- Folders, tags, favorites, and search.
- Asset type and aspect-ratio filters.
- “Used in projects” relationships.
- Rights/license status.
- Generated, uploaded, purchased, and shared categories.
- Duplicate detection.
- Version history.
- Signed private media URLs.
- One-click “Add to timeline.”

Every asset should show provenance: generated by which provider, created when, by whom, under which license, and whether it is cleared for commercial use.

### 7. Add a premium template marketplace

The marketplace can become a Nepal-focused creative economy. Sell:

- Nepali festival video packs.
- Restaurant and hotel advertisements.
- Real-estate promotion templates.
- School and training-center explainer packs.
- Tourism and trekking campaign kits.
- Local business social bundles.
- Voice packs and pronunciation dictionaries.
- Caption and lower-third packs.
- Royalty-cleared Nepali music and sound effects.

Add a live template preview, creator profile, license summary, commercial-use label, version history, ratings, and “use template” workflow. Keep license versioning immutable so a later license edit cannot alter an earlier purchase.

### 8. Introduce guided accessibility and simplicity modes

The platform is currently feature-rich but can feel complex. Add three modes:

| Mode | Intended user | UI behavior |
|---|---|---|
| Quick Create | Beginner or small business | One prompt, guided steps, minimal controls |
| Pro Studio | Creator or marketer | Full timeline, layers, branding, detailed controls |
| Team Workspace | Agency, school, NGO, company | Projects, approvals, roles, comments, history |

Add a persistent “What do you want to make?” entry point, contextual tooltips, empty-state examples, inline validation, autosave status, and a command palette. Never make users search through provider names before they know what result they want.

### 9. Add professional collaboration

For agencies and teams, add:

- Comments pinned to a scene or timeline timestamp.
- Mention notifications.
- Approval states: draft, review, changes requested, approved, published.
- Version comparison.
- Read-only client links with expiration.
- Role-based permissions.
- Activity history.
- Locking or conflict resolution for simultaneous editing.
- Client brand-kit restrictions.

The existing project/member/revision foundation is suitable for extending this, but durable database authority and conflict-safe writes should be completed first.

### 10. Build a durable render and delivery pipeline

Before promising MP4 rendering, implement a proper job system:

```text
Project timeline
   -> validation
   -> render job
   -> queue
   -> worker
   -> progress events
   -> preview artifact
   -> final MP4
   -> signed download URL
   -> retention/cleanup policy
```

The render system should support retries, cancellation, idempotency, progress percentages, estimated completion time, failed-job diagnostics, output-size limits, and a render history. Store job metadata in Supabase and media binaries in private object storage, not on ephemeral Space disk.

### 11. Add social publishing only after the render pipeline is mature

Direct YouTube, Facebook, Instagram, and TikTok publishing should come after:

- OAuth authorization with explicit scopes.
- User confirmation before publishing.
- Platform-specific aspect-ratio validation.
- Caption, title, hashtag, thumbnail, privacy, and scheduling controls.
- Retry and failure handling.
- Token encryption and rotation.
- Publication history and audit trail.
- Easy disconnect/revoke controls.

Until these controls exist, provide reliable export and user-initiated upload rather than a fragile “publish” button.

### 12. Add professional project analytics

For each project, show:

- Total production time.
- Number of scenes and assets.
- Audio/video duration.
- Estimated and actual credit usage.
- Render history.
- Most-used templates.
- Collaboration activity.
- Output formats.
- Download and publication history.

For administrators and investors, build durable reports for users, subscriptions, revenue, provider costs, gross margin, trial conversion, retention, failed jobs, and media-generation latency. These numbers must come from reconciled backend records, not browser counters.

### 13. Improve reliability and trust signals

A premium interface should always explain what is happening:

- “Preparing your script.”
- “Generating 4 visual variations.”
- “Creating narration, approximately 18 seconds.”
- “Rendering scene 4 of 8.”
- “Saved 12 seconds ago.”
- “Your project is recoverable.”
- “This asset is commercially cleared.”

Add provider health indicators, retry buttons, graceful fallback messages, queue position, estimated completion, and a clear error code for support. Do not show internal provider errors directly to users.

### 14. Premium design system improvements

The visual direction should use Nepal’s mountains and Himalayan atmosphere carefully rather than filling every surface with decoration. Recommended design language:

- Deep charcoal or midnight navy base.
- Everest-inspired blue, glacier cyan, prayer-flag saffron, and rhododendron accents.
- High-contrast typography with Devanagari support.
- One primary action per screen.
- Consistent icon family and icon-label pairing.
- Compact but readable side navigation.
- Foldable sidebar with remembered state.
- Mobile bottom navigation for core tools.
- Sticky preview and contextual inspector panel.
- Skeleton loading rather than blank space.
- Light theme with verified contrast ratios.
- Reduced-motion preference.
- Keyboard navigation and screen-reader labels.

The premium effect should come from **hierarchy, spacing, consistency, and confidence**, not excessive gradients or animation.

## Recommended implementation order

| Priority | Capability | Business value |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Guided Campaign Studio | Makes the platform understandable to first-time users |
| 2 | Professional timeline and autosave | Makes Video Studio genuinely usable |
| 3 | Voice Desk timing, pronunciation, and audio polish | Improves output quality and repeat usage |
| 4 | Brand kits and Nepal-first templates | Creates differentiation and recurring usage |
| 5 | Durable object storage and render jobs | Makes output reliable at scale |
| 6 | Asset Library and provenance | Improves reuse, trust, and marketplace value |
| 7 | Collaboration approvals and comments | Enables agencies, schools, NGOs, and teams |
| 8 | Analytics and provider cost accounting | Enables investor-grade operations |
| 9 | Social publishing | Adds distribution after reliability is proven |

## Strategic recommendation

The strongest product position is not “another AI generator.” It is:

> **Nepal’s guided AI production workspace for turning an idea into localized, branded, publish-ready campaigns.**

That position combines HamroAI for planning, Voice Desk for localized narration, Image Studio for visual continuity, Video Studio for assembly, the Asset Library for reuse, the Marketplace for templates and rights, and durable project/credit infrastructure for trust. Building this as one coherent workflow will make NepalAI Studio feel more premium than adding more disconnected provider buttons.

[CHAT #14] SENDER: user | TYPE: text | TIME: 2026-09-01T05:34:01.819Z
What additional features or UI refinements should be planned for the upcoming v1.30.0 release?

--------------------------------------------------
[CHAT #15] SENDER: assistant | TYPE: text | TIME: 2026-09-01T05:34:06.400Z
For v1.30.0, I recommend focusing on refinement and workflow quality rather than adding another large feature set. The priority should be making Video Studio feel faster, clearer, more trustworthy, and closer to a professional editor across desktop, tablet, and mobile.

--------------------------------------------------
[CHAT #16] SENDER: assistant | TYPE: text | TIME: 2026-09-01T05:34:30.018Z
## Recommended v1.30.0 scope

Version 1.30.0 should be a **Video Studio refinement and workflow-quality release**. The current foundation already includes a preview, timeline, scenes, captions, logos, motion, transitions, audio selection, draft recovery, and guided starters. The next release should make those capabilities feel more coherent and professional instead of introducing many unrelated tools.

### 1. Replace the current multi-card workflow with a clear three-step flow

Add a visible step indicator:

```text
1. Build the story  →  2. Polish the edit  →  3. Export and share
```

The first step should contain project name, format, scene source, and guided starter options. The second should focus on the monitor, timeline, inspector, text, logo, audio, and motion. The third should show quality, duration, estimated credits, output format, and render actions. This will reduce the feeling that users must understand every control before creating anything.

### 2. Add a proper selected-scene inspector

The existing scene controls should be consolidated into a stronger right-side inspector with tabs or sections:

| Inspector section | Controls |
|---|---|
| Transform | Fit, crop, position, scale, rotation, background treatment |
| Motion | Static, Ken Burns, pan, float, start/end direction |
| Timing | Duration, playback speed, fade in/out |
| Transition | Fade, slide, zoom, cut, transition duration |
| Text | Caption, position, style, animation, safe-area warning |
| Audio | Voice volume, music volume, fade, ducking |
| Brand | Logo, watermark, lower third, brand-safe placement |

The inspector should always make it obvious which scene is selected. On mobile, it can open as a bottom sheet instead of occupying a permanent side panel.

### 3. Add a compact timeline control bar

The timeline should receive a professional control bar containing:

- Zoom percentage and fit-to-project button.
- Snap toggle.
- Magnetic timeline toggle.
- Playhead timecode.
- Add scene button.
- Split-at-playhead button.
- Duplicate button.
- Delete button.
- Undo and redo.
- Track visibility and mute controls.
- Timeline help shortcut.

The current drag-and-drop behavior should also support clear drop indicators and an accessible keyboard alternative for reordering scenes.

### 4. Add scene thumbnails and visual continuity tools

For story, advertisement, information, and guide projects, add a continuity strip showing the selected visual style across scenes. Each scene could display a small label for:

- Character or subject.
- Location.
- Lighting.
- Visual style.
- Narration status.
- Caption status.
- Commercial-rights status.

Add a “Keep visual style consistent” switch for future image-generation workflows. This will be especially valuable for Nepali stories, tourism campaigns, product advertisements, and multi-scene explainers.

### 5. Improve preview quality and accessibility

The monitor should include:

- Fit-to-window and 100% preview controls.
- Fullscreen preview.
- Checkerboard or neutral background for transparent logos.
- Platform safe-area overlays for YouTube Shorts, Reels, TikTok, and YouTube landscape.
- Caption contrast warning.
- Logo contrast warning.
- Audio-muted indicator.
- Current scene and timecode always visible.
- Optional reduced-motion preview.

Add a clear contrast checker for text overlays. If a caption is too close to the edge or has weak contrast, show a non-blocking warning before rendering.

### 6. Introduce a real audio lane

The next release should make audio visible instead of treating it only as a picker. Add a compact audio lane with:

- Voiceover waveform or waveform placeholder.
- Music track label.
- Voice/music volume sliders.
- Music ducking amount.
- Fade in and fade out.
- Mute and solo controls.
- “Replace audio” action.
- Audio duration versus video duration warning.

The Voice Desk should be able to send a selected narration directly into the active Video Studio project with its language, duration, provider, and voice metadata preserved.

### 7. Add reusable text and brand overlays

Create a small overlay library with professional presets:

| Overlay | Use case |
|---|---|
| Headline banner | Opening title or key statement |
| Lower third | Person, place, role, or location |
| Phone and website strip | Business calls to action |
| Breaking-information ticker | News, alert, and information content |
| Step counter | Guides and tutorials |
| Quote card | Educational or inspirational content |
| End card | Logo, CTA, contact, and social handles |

Users should be able to save frequently used overlays as part of a Brand Kit.

### 8. Add an “estimated result” panel before render

Before the user clicks Render, display a compact preflight summary:

```text
Format: YouTube landscape 16:9
Resolution: 1080p
Frame rate: 30 fps
Duration: 42 seconds
Scenes: 6
Audio: Voice + music
Estimated credits: shown from backend rules
Estimated processing time: approximate range
Warnings: none
```

This will reduce failed renders and make credit usage more transparent. Estimated credits should eventually come from the backend rather than being calculated only in the browser.

### 9. Add project recovery and version history UI

The current local draft recovery should become more visible and trustworthy. Add:

- “Saved 12 seconds ago” status.
- Local draft versus Workspace draft distinction.
- Recoverable draft banner after reopening the page.
- Named version snapshots.
- Restore previous version.
- Duplicate project.
- Discard changes with confirmation.
- Recovery warning if the project contains temporary browser-only media.

This is especially important before moving toward durable Supabase-backed project authority.

### 10. Improve responsive layouts deliberately

For desktop, retain the monitor/timeline/inspector composition. For tablet, stack the inspector below the monitor and keep the timeline horizontally scrollable. For mobile, use:

- Preview at the top.
- A sticky compact transport bar.
- Horizontally scrollable timeline.
- Bottom-sheet inspector.
- Large touch targets.
- Simplified quick actions.
- No accidental page navigation when number keys or space are pressed inside the editor.

Test at minimum on a narrow mobile viewport, a tablet viewport, a 1366px desktop viewport, and a wide monitor. The existing platform has previously experienced readability and keyboard-navigation issues, so this should be a formal release gate.

### 11. Add a better empty state and guided examples

The empty Video Studio should not appear like a blank technical editor. Show three illustrated paths:

- **Create from an idea:** opens Campaign Builder.
- **Start with images:** opens the media picker.
- **Use a professional template:** opens the template browser.

Include example use cases such as “Nepali tourism reel,” “restaurant promotion,” “school announcement,” “product advertisement,” and “three-step public information guide.”

### 12. Add controlled micro-interactions

Use subtle feedback only where it improves clarity:

- Timeline drop indicator.
- Selected-scene highlight.
- Autosave confirmation.
- Render-stage progress.
- Toast after adding or duplicating a scene.
- Inline warning for missing media.
- Skeleton state while assets load.

Avoid excessive animation. The Himalayan aesthetic should come through in restrained color accents, map or mountain-inspired dividers, strong spacing, and Nepali/Devanagari typography support—not through decorative motion everywhere.

## Suggested v1.30.0 acceptance criteria

| Area | Release requirement |
|---|---|
| Creation | A new user can create a starter project without reading instructions |
| Editing | A user can select, reorder, duplicate, delete, and configure a scene without ambiguity |
| Timeline | Drag-and-drop and keyboard reordering both work |
| Preview | The selected scene, timecode, format, safe area, and overlays are visible |
| Audio | Voice and music roles are understandable and independently controllable |
| Export | The user sees a preflight summary before rendering |
| Recovery | A draft can be recovered or restored without silently overwriting Workspace data |
| Responsive UI | The editor remains usable on desktop, tablet, and mobile widths |
| Accessibility | Focus states, labels, contrast warnings, and reduced-motion behavior are present |
| Compatibility | Existing backend endpoints, credits, quotas, and project-save behavior remain intact |

## Recommended implementation sequence

**v1.30.0-A — Editor shell refinement:** Add the three-step workflow, compact timeline toolbar, stronger inspector, scene selection clarity, and improved responsive behavior.

**v1.30.0-B — Audio and overlay polish:** Add visible audio controls, overlay presets, brand-kit connections, and text-safe-area warnings.

**v1.30.0-C — Preflight and recovery:** Add render summary, credit/time estimate display, project recovery banner, version snapshots, and failure explanations.

**v1.30.0-D — QA hardening:** Test desktop, tablet, mobile, light theme, dark theme, Devanagari text, keyboard shortcuts, empty state, missing media, long projects, and interrupted renders.

The most valuable goal for v1.30.0 is this: **a first-time user should be able to create a polished short video in a few guided decisions, while an experienced user can still access professional timeline controls when needed.**

## v1.30.0-A completed

The Video Studio editor shell has been upgraded with the first professional workflow layer:

| Area | Completed refinement |
|---|---|
| Workflow | Added **Build the story → Polish the edit → Export & share** stepper |
| Timeline | Added Preview, previous/next scene, Duplicate, Delete, Split, Snap, Zoom, and Fit controls |
| Editing | Added split-at-playhead behavior for turning one scene into two editable clips |
| Navigation | Added clear selected-scene focus styling and keyboard-accessible controls |
| Responsive UI | Added mobile/tablet toolbar adaptation and horizontally usable timeline behavior |
| Existing features | Preserved scenes, captions, logos, ticker, motion, transitions, audio, drafts, quotas, credits, Workspace, and backend contracts |
| Release markers | Updated frontend and Director Desk markers to **v1.30.0-A** |

Replace all three files together:

Then perform a hard refresh. No Hugging Face backend update is required for v1.30.0-A.

[CHAT #20] SENDER: user | TYPE: text | TIME: 2026-09-01T05:42:32.113Z
What features and UI improvements are planned for the next v1.31.0 release?

--------------------------------------------------
[CHAT #21] SENDER: assistant | TYPE: text | TIME: 2026-09-01T05:42:50.391Z
## Recommended v1.31.0 direction

After v1.30.0-A established the workflow stepper and timeline toolbar, v1.31.0 should focus on **professional media control, intelligent preflight validation, and project continuity**. It should make the editor feel more like a dependable production tool without yet depending on the unfinished server-side MP4 pipeline.

### 1. Build a real scene inspector

Replace the current scattered scene controls with a focused inspector that always reflects the selected clip.

| Section | Planned controls |
|---|---|
| Transform | Fit, crop, scale, position, rotation, background treatment |
| Motion | Static, Ken Burns, pan, float, direction, intensity |
| Timing | Duration, speed, fade in, fade out |
| Transition | Type, duration, preview transition |
| Text | Caption, position, style, animation, alignment |
| Brand | Logo, watermark, opacity, safe placement |
| Audio | Voice level, music level, mute, fade, ducking |

The inspector should display a clear “Scene X selected” state and show an empty-state explanation when nothing is selected.

### 2. Add a visible audio lane

Audio is currently an important part of the product but should become more visible in the timeline. v1.31.0 should add a dedicated audio lane with voiceover and music rows, mute/solo controls, volume sliders, fade controls, duration mismatch warnings, and music-ducking feedback.

The Voice Desk should be able to send a narration take directly into the active video project while preserving language, voice, duration, and provider metadata.

### 3. Add preflight validation before rendering

Before rendering, show a production-readiness panel rather than allowing preventable failures.

```text
Project ready for render
✓ All scenes contain media
✓ Format selected
✓ Captions inside safe area
✓ Logo contrast acceptable
✓ Audio duration matches project
✓ Output quality selected
Estimated duration: 42 seconds
Estimated credits: calculated from backend rules
Estimated processing time: shown as a range
```

Warnings should be actionable. For example, “Scene 3 has no image” should include a direct **Add media** action rather than only showing an error message.

### 4. Improve media replacement and asset continuity

Each scene should support **Replace media**, **Remove media**, **Open in Asset Library**, and **Generate variation**. Add a continuity panel that records visual style, subject, location, lighting, and character notes across scenes.

This will be especially useful for Nepali stories, tourism campaigns, product promotions, educational explainers, and multi-scene advertisements where unrelated visuals currently reduce quality.

### 5. Introduce overlay presets

Create a reusable overlay library for common production elements:

- Headline banner.
- Lower third.
- Phone and website strip.
- Information ticker.
- Step counter.
- Quote card.
- End card.
- Logo watermark.
- Festival greeting frame.
- Tourism location label.

Presets should respect the active Brand Kit and platform safe areas. Users should be able to save customized overlays for reuse.

### 6. Add project version history

Extend the current draft recovery into visible project history. Add named snapshots, restore, duplicate project, discard changes, local draft versus Workspace draft labels, and a timestamp such as “Saved 18 seconds ago.”

A warning should appear when a draft contains browser-only temporary media that cannot be recovered after the browser session ends.

### 7. Improve timeline precision

The timeline should receive professional editing behavior:

| Improvement | Purpose |
|---|---|
| Snap toggle | Align clip boundaries cleanly |
| Split at playhead | Divide scenes precisely |
| Trim handles | Adjust duration visually |
| Ripple reorder | Move scenes without losing timing context |
| Magnetic placement | Prevent accidental gaps |
| Timecode entry | Jump to an exact position |
| Fit-to-window | See the complete project quickly |
| Track controls | Mute, hide, and lock tracks |
| Keyboard alternative | Reorder scenes without drag-and-drop |

Add visual indicators for the playhead, selected clip, drop target, and current scene boundary.

### 8. Add platform-aware delivery profiles

Create saved delivery profiles for:

- YouTube landscape, 16:9.
- YouTube Shorts, 9:16.
- Instagram Reels, 9:16.
- TikTok, 9:16.
- Facebook landscape and portrait.
- Square social post, 1:1.
- Social portrait, 4:5.

Each profile should remember aspect ratio, safe-area guides, caption treatment, frame rate, quality, and recommended duration. The user should be able to create one project and prepare multiple output variants.

### 9. Add better preview controls

The monitor should support fullscreen, fit-to-window, 100% view, muted preview, safe-area overlays, caption preview, logo preview, and platform frame overlays. Add a visual difference between **preview quality** and **final export quality** so users understand why the monitor may look lighter than the final result.

### 10. Improve accessibility and internationalization

v1.31.0 should formally test:

- Nepali Devanagari captions.
- Hindi captions.
- Mixed-language brand names.
- Keyboard-only editing.
- Screen-reader labels.
- Visible focus rings.
- High-contrast light theme.
- High-contrast dark theme.
- Reduced-motion mode.
- Touch targets on mobile.
- Text overflow for long Nepali phrases.

Add character-count and safe-area indicators for captions. Show warnings when text is too long for a selected platform or frame.

### 11. Add render history and clearer result handling

Even before the durable render worker is implemented, the frontend can prepare a stronger render history panel containing project name, output profile, requested time, completion status, duration, file type, download action, and retry action.

Do not present a failed browser render as a successful finished project. The result screen should distinguish between preview-ready, downloadable, and publish-ready states.

### 12. Improve the mobile editing experience

On mobile, use a bottom-sheet inspector, compact transport controls, a horizontally scrollable timeline, and large touch targets. Keep Preview, Add Scene, Inspector, and Render available as persistent primary actions.

The mobile interface should not display every advanced option at once. Place less frequent controls behind expandable sections while keeping the main workflow visible.

## Proposed v1.31.0 milestones

| Milestone | Scope |
|---|---|
| v1.31.0-A | Real selected-scene inspector and improved transform/motion/timing controls |
| v1.31.0-B | Dedicated audio lane, volume controls, fade, mute, and voiceover handoff |
| v1.31.0-C | Preflight validation, safe-area warnings, credit/time summary, and output profiles |
| v1.31.0-D | Version history, render history, mobile refinement, accessibility, and QA hardening |

## Recommended priority

The highest-value sequence is **inspector first, audio lane second, preflight validation third, then history and accessibility**. These improvements directly address the main weaknesses of a basic editor: users cannot easily understand what is selected, audio is not sufficiently visual, preventable render problems are discovered too late, and draft safety is not obvious.

The strategic goal for v1.31.0 should be:

> **Every scene is understandable, every important control is discoverable, every render is checked before it starts, and every project is recoverable.**

Server-side MP4 rendering, private object storage, and direct social publishing should remain separate backend milestones rather than being simulated as frontend-only features.

| Captions | Per-scene caption editing with safe-area guidance |
| Empty state | Clear instructions when no scene is selected |
| Timeline connection | Inspector changes immediately update the selected scene, timeline, and preview |
| Navigation | Focus selected clip from the inspector |
| Responsive UI | Inspector stacks cleanly on tablet and mobile widths |
| Compatibility | Existing render, credits, quotas, Workspace save, draft recovery, and backend contracts preserved |

[CHAT #25] SENDER: user | TYPE: text | TIME: 2026-09-01T05:45:18.894Z
What features and improvements are planned for the subsequent v1.32.0 release?

--------------------------------------------------
[CHAT #26] SENDER: assistant | TYPE: text | TIME: 2026-09-01T05:45:23.202Z
For v1.32.0, I recommend moving from scene-level editing to production readiness: a visible audio lane, stronger render preflight, multi-format delivery profiles, improved asset continuity, and project/version safety. The release should make the studio feel dependable for real campaigns without prematurely simulating backend capabilities that still require the durable render pipeline.

--------------------------------------------------
[CHAT #27] SENDER: assistant | TYPE: text | TIME: 2026-09-01T05:45:43.408Z
## Recommended v1.32.0 direction

Version 1.32.0 should focus on **production readiness and delivery confidence**. v1.31.0-A made individual scene editing clearer; v1.32.0 should connect those scenes into a more complete, verifiable, and reusable video-production workflow.

### 1. Add a dedicated audio lane

The timeline should show voiceover and music as visible tracks rather than treating audio as a separate picker.

| Audio capability | Planned behavior |
|---|---|
| Voiceover track | Show narration assignment, duration, language, voice, and provider |
| Music track | Show sound-bed name, license status, duration, and trim state |
| Volume | Separate voice and music controls |
| Ducking | Automatically lower music beneath narration |
| Fade | Fade in, fade out, and crossfade controls |
| Mute/solo | Preview each audio track independently |
| Mismatch warning | Identify audio shorter or longer than the project |
| Replacement | Replace a track without rebuilding the timeline |

The Voice Desk should send a finished narration take directly into the active project with its metadata preserved.

### 2. Add render preflight validation

Before rendering, show a production-readiness summary with actionable warnings.

```text
Ready to render
✓ Every scene has media
✓ Project format selected
✓ Captions within safe area
✓ Logo placement is valid
✓ Audio duration is compatible
✓ Output profile selected

Estimated output: 42 seconds · 1080p · YouTube landscape
Estimated credits: calculated from account rules
Estimated processing time: displayed as a range
```

Warnings should link directly to the relevant control. For example, a missing image should provide **Add media**, and an unsafe caption should provide **Move caption to safe area**.

### 3. Introduce platform delivery profiles

Create reusable profiles for YouTube, Shorts, Reels, TikTok, Facebook, square posts, and 4:5 social content. Each profile should remember aspect ratio, safe-area guides, caption treatment, frame rate, quality, and recommended output settings.

A strong v1.32.0 workflow would allow a user to configure one master project and prepare multiple delivery variants without rebuilding the story.

### 4. Add output-variant management

Add a delivery panel where users can choose:

- YouTube landscape.
- Vertical short-form.
- Square social.
- Social portrait.
- Custom format.

Each variant should show its own format, duration, quality, captions, logo position, and readiness status. The project should distinguish the master edit from delivery-specific adjustments.

### 5. Improve media replacement and asset continuity

Every scene should provide clear actions for **Replace media**, **Remove media**, **Open in Asset Library**, **Generate variation**, and **Duplicate as new scene**.

Add continuity metadata for subject, location, visual style, lighting, color treatment, and character. This is important for Nepal-first campaigns where visual consistency across multiple generated images strongly affects perceived quality.

### 6. Add overlay presets and Brand Kit integration

Introduce reusable production overlays:

| Preset | Typical use |
|---|---|
| Headline banner | Opening message or key statement |
| Lower third | Person, place, role, or location |
| Phone/website strip | Business contact and CTA |
| Information ticker | Awareness, news, and alert content |
| Step counter | Tutorials and guides |
| Tourism label | Location and destination promotion |
| Festival frame | Dashain, Tihar, Teej, New Year, and local events |
| End card | Logo, CTA, contact, and social handles |

Presets should inherit active brand colors, logo, typography, and contact details from Brand Kit.

### 7. Add caption safety and localization tools

Provide a caption panel with character count, line count, safe-area preview, maximum recommended length, and language-aware wrapping. It should support Nepali Devanagari, Hindi, English, and mixed brand names without causing layout overflow.

Add presets for documentary, business, news, tutorial, festival, and social captions. Show a warning when a caption is too long for the chosen aspect ratio or when contrast is too weak.

### 8. Add project version history

Extend current draft recovery into a visible version system:

- Autosave timestamps.
- Local draft versus Workspace draft labels.
- Named snapshots.
- Restore version.
- Duplicate project.
- Discard changes with confirmation.
- Recovery notice after browser interruption.
- Warning for temporary browser-only media.

No version restore should silently overwrite the current project. The user should see which version will be restored before confirming.

### 9. Add a render history panel

The result area should show previous render attempts with project name, profile, requested time, completion state, duration, file type, and available actions. Keep the states distinct:

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Draft ready | Timeline is editable but not rendered |
| Preflight warning | User must review an issue |
| Rendering | Job is actively processing |
| Preview ready | Browser preview is available |
| Download ready | A downloadable output exists |
| Failed | Output was not created and can be retried |
| Published | A social platform accepted the output |

This avoids presenting a partial or failed render as a finished production asset.

### 10. Improve accessibility and mobile editing

Formally test keyboard-only editing, screen-reader labels, visible focus states, reduced-motion mode, dark/light contrast, touch targets, and long Devanagari captions.

For mobile, use a bottom-sheet inspector, persistent Preview/Add Scene/Inspector/Render actions, and a horizontally scrollable timeline. Advanced options should be grouped into expandable sections rather than shown simultaneously.

### 11. Add guided production templates

Expand starter sequences into complete templates for:

- Nepali tourism reels.
- Restaurant and hotel promotions.
- School and training-center announcements.
- Local business advertisements.
- NGO and public-awareness guides.
- Festival greetings.
- Product showcase videos.
- Tips-and-tricks explainers.

A template should include recommended scene structure, caption style, timing, overlay positions, and CTA—not only empty scene placeholders.

### 12. Add business-safe asset provenance

Every media item should show whether it is generated, uploaded, original, purchased, or shared; whether it has commercial-use clearance; and when it was created. This should be visible in the scene inspector and Asset Library before export.

This is important for the future marketplace and for business customers who need confidence that their campaign assets can be used commercially.

## Suggested v1.32.0 milestones

| Milestone | Scope |
|---|---|
| v1.32.0-A | Dedicated audio lane, voice/music controls, ducking, fade, mute, and Voice Desk handoff |
| v1.32.0-B | Render preflight, actionable warnings, caption safety, logo checks, and estimate summary |
| v1.32.0-C | Platform delivery profiles, output variants, overlay presets, and Brand Kit inheritance |
| v1.32.0-D | Version history, render history, mobile refinement, accessibility, and provenance QA |

## Backend boundary

The frontend can prepare the audio-lane model, preflight UI, delivery profiles, and render-history interface. However, reliable final MP4 generation still requires the Hugging Face backend render-job pipeline, private object storage, signed URLs, retries, cancellation, idempotency, and durable Supabase metadata.

Direct YouTube, Facebook, Instagram, and TikTok publishing should remain gated until authentication scopes, token security, platform-specific validation, publication history, and failure recovery are fully implemented.

## Strategic goal

> **v1.32.0 should make every project production-ready before render, every output profile intentional, and every important media decision visible.**

The highest-value order is **audio lane first, preflight validation second, delivery profiles third, and history/provenance fourth**. This sequence improves the user experience immediately while preparing the platform for the later durable rendering and publishing phases.

[CHAT #30] SENDER: assistant | TYPE: text | TIME: 2026-09-01T05:49:30.224Z
## v1.32.0-A completed

The Video Studio now has a visible audio workflow with a professional mixer and separate Voiceover/Music Bed presentation.

### Implemented

| Area | Enhancement |
|---|---|
| Timeline | Added separate Voiceover and Music Bed lanes |
| Audio Mixer | Added voice volume and music volume controls |
| Mute controls | Added independent voice and music mute/unmute buttons |
| Ducking | Added Auto-duck music under voice toggle and status feedback |
| Fade | Added None, Soft, and Cinematic fade selection |
| Track roles | Uploaded audio is treated as Voiceover; original sound beds are treated as Music Bed |
| Readiness | Added Audio Ready / No Audio Selected status |
| Metadata | Added selected-track labels and volume/mute state in the audio lane |
| Rendering | Connected selected audio through a browser gain node before the existing MediaRecorder stream |
| Responsive UI | Added mobile-friendly stacked audio controls |
| Compatibility | Preserved existing audio picker, sound shelf, timeline, preview, rendering, credits, quotas, Workspace, and backend behavior |

### QA completed

`node --check app.js` passed. Structural checks confirmed the audio mixer, audio-lane renderer, mixer update handler, gain-node integration, `1.32.0-A` marker, and served frontend. The local frontend returned HTTP 200.

### cPanel upload

Replace these three files together:

1. `index.html`
2. `app.js`
3. `styles.css`

Then perform a hard refresh. No Hugging Face backend update is required for this release.

### Important limitation

The current backend/browser render contract still supports one selected audio URL at a time. This release provides separate role presentation and gain control for that selected track. True simultaneous independent voiceover and music binary mixing requires the planned durable render-job and backend media pipeline.

### Important limitation

Also, the current browser renderer still outputs WebM and supports one selected audio URL at a time. Full professional AI background removal, independent multi-track mixing, MP4 rendering, and durable media storage require later backend work.

Both capabilities should be implemented as **durable asynchronous jobs** with private media storage, idempotent requests, explicit credit reservation, progress reporting, retry policies, and audit records.\n\nThe safe production sequence is:\n\n```text\nAuthenticated user\n  → upload media to private storage\n  → create durable job record\n  → reserve credits\n  → queue work\n  → provider/worker processing\n  → validate output\n  → write artifact metadata\n  → signed download URL\n  → frontend refreshes job status\n```\n\nSupabase production writes should remain gated until identity and credit parity are complete. ### Safety status

All new routes, database access, storage access, and worker execution are **disabled by default**. The package does not activate Supabase production writes or charge user credits. The migration must be applied and the private bucket, identity mapping, provider adapter, credit reservation logic, and FFmpeg runtime must be verified before enabling the feature flags.

### Verification

Syntax checks passed for the modified server and all new modules. The local review copy could not complete a full backend startup because it is missing the pre-existing `azureClient.js` dependency. This is a limitation of the local review folder, not a syntax error in the new package. Validate startup against the complete Hugging Face Space file set before deployment.

### Important implementation boundary

The worker is intentionally a guarded foundation at this stage. It currently claims jobs and records explicit `BACKGROUND_WORKER_STORAGE_NOT_WIRED` or `MP4_COMPOSER_NOT_ENABLED` failures until the real private-object download/upload and FFmpeg composition stages are added. Do not enable the worker in production yet.

[CHAT #50] SENDER: assistant | TYPE: text | TIME: 2026-09-02T04:58:09.444Z
Implemented the real worker logic and prepared the updated backend files.

### Implemented in `mediaWorker.js`

- Downloads private source objects from Supabase Storage.
- Validates ownership through `media_objects` lookup.
- Validates object size and SHA-256 checksum.
- Processes background-removal jobs through the configured provider adapter.
- Uploads transparent PNG derivatives to private storage.
- Creates durable media-object and artifact records.
- Resolves credit reservations on success or failure.
- Composes image and video scenes with FFmpeg.
- Supports captions, basic emoji overlays, motion treatment, optional logos, scene timing, and transitions through the versioned manifest.
- Mixes voice and music inputs with gain controls and `amix`.
- Produces H.264/AAC MP4 with `+faststart`.
- Validates output size, duration, dimensions, codec probe, and SHA-256 checksum.
- Uploads validated MP4 artifacts to private storage.
- Preserves worker leases, cleanup, explicit error codes, retryability, and feature flags.

### Supporting updates

- Extended `mediaJobStore.js` with source-object lookup, artifact creation, media-object creation, and credit-reservation resolution.
- Extended `privateMediaStorage.js` with private object download.
- Corrected FFmpeg audio filter assembly and scene/audio/logo input indexing.
- Added migration `006_media_jobs_and_artifacts.sql`.
- Added sanitized `.env.media-jobs.example`.
- Added the `worker:media` package script.
- Added a local FFmpeg smoke test, which successfully produced an MP4.

### QA status

Syntax checks passed for `server.js`, `mediaWorker.js`, `mediaJobStore.js`, `privateMediaStorage.js`, `mediaJobRoutes.js`, and `backgroundRemovalProvider.js`. FFmpeg and FFprobe are available in the current runtime, and the composition smoke test returned `FFMPEG_SMOKE_OK`.

A complete local server startup could not be performed because the review copy is missing the pre-existing `azureClient.js` dependency. Validate startup against the complete Hugging Face Space file set before deployment.

### Do not enable yet

Keep these flags disabled until Supabase migration, private bucket setup, provider configuration, authenticated job creation, credit reservation wiring, and complete Hugging Face startup QA pass:

```text
MEDIA_JOBS_API_ENABLED=false
MEDIA_JOB_DB_ENABLED=false
PRIVATE_MEDIA_STORAGE_ENABLED=false
MEDIA_WORKER_ENABLED=false
BACKGROUND_REMOVAL_MODE=off
RENDER_MP4_MODE=off
```

The package is now a real processing implementation, but production activation still requires environment and end-to-end integration validation.

## Azure primary image and video integration prepared

I integrated the user-provided Azure resources as the primary generation paths.

### Hugging Face backend

The backend now includes:

- `azureMediaProvider.js` for MAI Image 2.5 Pro and Sora 2.
- `azureMediaRoutes.js` with:
  - `POST /api/images/azure`
  - `POST /api/video/azure`
  - `GET /api/video/azure/status/:jobId`
- Updated `server.js` route registration and provider health reporting.
- Updated package version to `1.9.0`.
- Sanitized Azure environment template.

Image generation uses the documented MAI endpoint with the server-side `api-key` header and supports base64 image responses. Sora uses the documented asynchronous create → poll → download flow with `api-version=preview` by default.

### cPanel frontend

The frontend now:

- Selects **Azure MAI Image 2.5 Pro** as the default Image Studio provider.
- Converts aspect-ratio selections into Azure width/height values.
- Accepts base64-generated images.
- Displays preview versus premium quality guidance.
- Adds a **Generate AI Clip — Azure Sora 2** action inside Video Studio.
- Supports English and Nepali prompts.
- Supports vertical, landscape, and square video formats.
- Polls the Sora job and adds the returned clip to the current Video Studio timeline.
- Supports Admin Workspace testing through the existing server-authorized admin headers.
- Updates the frontend marker to `1.33.0-A`.

### Quality and pricing gates

Free users can request low-cost preview quality. Premium image/video generation requires a paid entitlement. Admin Workspace requests bypass credit deduction for testing through the existing admin mechanism. Existing normal-user authentication, daily trial limits, and credit checks remain in place.

### QA status

Backend and frontend syntax checks passed. Azure provider-route checks passed. The frontend checks confirmed the Azure image route, Sora video route, default provider, bilingual prompt field, and release marker.

A real Azure live-generation test was not executed because the secret value was not provided in chat—and it should not be provided in chat. Add the key only as a Hugging Face Space Secret, then test through Admin Workspace.

Upload the backend files to the Hugging Face Space and upload the three frontend files to cPanel:

1. `index.html`
2. `app.js`
3. `styles.css`

    "contentPreview": "# NepalAI Studio Azure primary image/video integration v1.33.0\n\n## Backend changes\n\nThe Hugging Face backend now includes `azureMediaProvider.js` and `azureMediaRoutes.js`. The new routes are:\n\n- `POST /api/images/azure` for Azure MAI image generation.\n- `POST /api/video/azure` for asynchronous Azure Sora 2 job creation.\n- `GET /api/video/azure/status/:jobId` for polling and binary video retrieval.\n\nThe image adapter uses `POST {endpoint}/mai/v1/images/generations` with the `api-key` header and reads `data[].b64_json`. The Sora adapter defaults to the documented Azure Foundry v1 preview job flow: create at `/openai/v1/video/generations/jobs?api-version=preview`, poll the job, and retrieve generated video content after success.\n\n## Hugging Face Space Secrets\n\nAdd these values as Space Secrets. Never add them to cPanel files:\n\n```text\nAZURE_API_KEY=your-secret-value\nAZURE_MAI_IMAGE_ENDPOINT=https://nepalai.services.ai.azure.com\nAZURE_MAI_IMAGE_MODEL=MAI-Image-2.5-Pro\nAZURE_MAI_IMAGE_PREVIEW_MODEL=MAI-Image-2.5-Pro\nAZURE_SORA_ENDPOINT=https://prakashsuvedi-7749-resource.services.ai.azure.com\nAZURE_SORA_DEPLOYMENT_NAME=sora-2\nAZURE_SORA_MODEL=sora-2\nAZURE_SORA_API_VERSION=preview\nAZURE_SORA_API_STYLE=foundry-v1\n```\n\nThe frontend never receives `AZURE_API_KEY`. The backend is the only component that calls Azure.\n\n## Quality policy\n\n`preview` is limited to low-cost image/video generation. Premium image quality requires a paid entitlement and uses the configured MAI Pro model. Premium Sora generation is limited to 20 seconds in the application contract; preview video generation is limited to 4 seconds. Admin Workspace requests bypass credit deduction for testing through the existing server-authorized admin token.\n\n## Frontend changes\n\nThe cPanel frontend now selects Azure MAI as the default Image Studio provider, converts the existing aspect-ratio selector into Azure width/height values, accepts base64 image responses, labels preview/premium quality, and adds a Video Studio “Generate AI clip” action. The clip creator accepts English or Nepali prompts, allows preview/premium quality, supports vertical, landscape, and square output, polls the Sora job, and adds the returned clip to the current Video Studio timeline.\n\n## Deployment order\n\n1. Deploy the backend files to the Hugging Face Space.\n2. Add the Space Secrets above.\n3. Restart the Space.\n4. Use Admin Workspace to test one preview image and one 4-second preview video.\n5. Confirm the Azure deployment name and exact Sora response contract from the returned job.\n6. Test failed, throttled, unauthorized, and insufficient-credit responses.\n7. Upload the cPanel files together: `index.html`, `app.js`, and `styles.css`.\n8. Test Image Studio and Video Studio from a normal Google account.\n\n## Current limitations and gates\n\nMAI Image 2.5 Pro and Sora 2 are documented as preview offerings. Do not advertise production SLA guarantees until live tests pass. The frontend timeline currently receives generated video a",

sorry it was not MAI Image 2.5 Pro , https://prakashsuvedi-7749-resource.services.ai.azure.com/api/projects/prakashsuvedi-7749  it was gpt-image-1.5 , 
import base64
from openai import OpenAI
from azure.identity import DefaultAzureCredential, get_bearer_token_provider

endpoint = "https://prakashsuvedi-7749-resource.services.ai.azure.com/openai/v1"
deployment_name = "gpt-image-1.5"
token_provider = get_bearer_token_provider(DefaultAzureCredential(), "https://ai.azure.com/.default")

client = OpenAI(
    base_url=endpoint,
    api_key=token_provider
)

img = client.images.generate(
    model=deployment_name,
    prompt="A cute baby polar bear",
    n=1,
    size="1024x1024",
)

image_bytes = base64.b64decode(img.data[0].b64_json)
with open("output.png", "wb") as f:
    f.write(image_bytes) please replace that with this . this is what working while i tested.

```text
Endpoint: https://prakashsuvedi-7749-resource.services.ai.azure.com/openai/v1

Model:   gpt-image-1.5
Body:    prompt, n: 1, size
Header:  api-key, server-side only
```

The adapter now supports `AZURE_IMAGE_AUTH_MODE=api-key` by default and can also use bearer mode if your Azure resource requires token authentication. Sora 2 video configuration remains unchanged.

### Corrected frontend

- Provider card now displays **Azure gpt-image-1.5**.

- Existing preview/premium quality gates remain active.

## Recommended Docker fix

Change the backend Docker base image from Node 18 to Node 20 or 22. For example:

```dockerfile
FROM node:20-slim
```

or:

```dockerfile
FROM node:22-slim
```

Node 20 is the safer compatibility choice for the current backend unless your Hugging Face Space already supports Node 22.

## What to check next

If the Space is still not opening, the problem is probably **after the build**, during application startup. Please open the Hugging Face **Runtime Logs** and look for lines after the image build, especially:

```text
npm start
Error: Cannot find module ...
Error: listen EADDRINUSE ...
Supabase connection error
Azure provider error
```

The build output you pasted is successful. Do not use `npm audit fix --force` yet because it can introduce breaking changes. First update the Node base image, redeploy, and inspect the runtime logs if the Space remains unavailable.

[CHAT #69] SENDER: assistant | TYPE: text | TIME: 2026-09-04T02:44:36.086Z
Your diagnosis is partly correct: the `404` confirms the deployed Hugging Face backend still does not expose `/api/video/azure`, which means the corrected backend files were not fully deployed or the Space is running an older commit. The `402` on images is an entitlement/credit response from the backend, while the `400` is request validation—neither is an Azure endpoint redirect failure. I’ll trace the deployed route and payload contract, then provide a precise fix without bypassing paid-credit or admin authorization.

--------------------------------------------------
[CHAT #70] SENDER: assistant | TYPE: text | TIME: 2026-09-04T02:46:02.326Z
## Confirmed root cause

I checked the live endpoint:

```json
{
  "status": "ok",
  "version": "1.7.0",
  "azureMaiImage": true,
  "azureSoraVideo": true
}
```

The live Hugging Face Space is still running **backend v1.7.0**, not the corrected backend package v1.9.0. That explains the behavior:

| Error | Actual cause |
|---|---|
| `POST /api/video/azure → 404` | The running v1.7.0 backend does not have the new Sora route registered |
| `POST /api/images/azure → 402` | The request is reaching an older credit/entitlement gate; premium quality requires paid entitlement or a valid Admin Workspace token |
| `POST /api/images/azure → 400` | The old image route contract does not match the new gpt-image-1.5 payload/quality contract |

The endpoint URLs are not the primary problem at this stage. The new backend code is simply not running in the Space.

## Required fix

Upload the complete contents of the attached `hf_backend_v1.9.1_complete.zip` into the **same root directory** where `server.js` currently exists. Do not leave it as an extra nested folder such as:

```text
/app/hf_backend_v1.9.1_complete/server.js
```

The correct layout must be:

```text
/app/server.js
/app/azureClient.js
/app/azureMediaProvider.js
/app/azureMediaRoutes.js
/app/mediaJobRoutes.js
/app/mediaJobStore.js
/app/privateMediaStorage.js
/app/mediaWorker.js
/app/package.json
/app/package-lock.json
```

Most importantly, ensure these files are present in the deployed Space:

```text
server.js
azureClient.js
azureMediaProvider.js
azureMediaRoutes.js
mediaJobRoutes.js
mediaJobStore.js
privateMediaStorage.js
backgroundRemovalProvider.js
mediaWorker.js
package.json
package-lock.json
```

Then commit the files and wait for the build to finish. The health endpoint must report the new release rather than `1.7.0` before testing generation.

## Test order after redeployment

1. Open `/api/health` and confirm the backend version is no longer `1.7.0`.
2. Test a preview image from Admin Workspace.
3. Test a 4-second preview video from Admin Workspace.
4. Test normal-user preview generation.
5. Test premium generation only with a paid entitlement.
6. Confirm that failed requests do not consume credits.

Do not bypass the `402` response by removing credit checks. First get the corrected backend running; then the admin token and quality gates can be tested against the correct route implementation.

```json
}
```

I also verified that the live server returns:

```text
Cannot GET /api/video/azure
```

That confirms the Sora route is absent from the running deployment. Therefore, the current failures are not caused by the Azure project ID or your Sora/gpt-image-1.5 resource URLs. The Space is serving the old backend.

### Why each action fails

| Action | Current result | Verified reason |
|---|---:|---|
| Sora generation | 404 | Live backend v1.7.0 has no `/api/video/azure` route |
| gpt-image premium | 402 | Existing old credit/entitlement gate rejects the request before the corrected adapter runs |
| gpt-image request | 400 | Old image route does not understand the corrected gpt-image-1.5 contract and payload |
| Admin testing | Controlled mode | The running frontend/backend combination is not using the new admin-aware provider flow, or the admin session headers are not reaching the new routes |
